<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a positive answer to the constant-error formulation of the Aaronson–Kuperberg unitary synthesis problem. For every <i>n</i>, a quantum oracle circuit generated in polynomial time from <i>n</i> alone can approximate the channel of every <i>n</i>-qubit unitary to full diamond-norm error at most 1/2, after a suitable Boolean oracle is chosen. Using the fixed gates $`H,T,T^\dagger,\mathrm{CNOT}`$, the circuit has polynomially many qubits, elementary gates, and oracle calls, and its oracle queries have polynomial length. The oracle may depend on the target unitary; the theorem does not give an efficient classical procedure for constructing it.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are a polynomial $p$ and a deterministic classical algorithm which, on input $1^n$ for any integer $n\ge1$, outputs in time at most $p(n)$ a quantum oracle circuit $A_n$ with the following properties. The circuit uses the gates $H,T,T^\dagger,\mathrm{CNOT}$ and a single Boolean oracle of input length $m(n)\le p(n)$. Its total number of qubits, elementary gates, and oracle calls are each at most $p(n)$. For every unitary $U\in U(2^n)$ there is a function $f:\{0,1\}^{m(n)}\to\{0,1\}$ for which the output channel $\Phi_{n,f}$ of $A_n^{O_f}$ satisfies $$\lVert \Phi_{n,f}-\mathcal U\rVert_\diamond\le\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: The generator depends only on the input width. A target-dependent Boolean oracle supplies the description needed to approximate every target channel, with a worst-case guarantee that includes entangled inputs.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-slot-preparation)): For every $u\ge1$ there is a skeleton of $u+1$ slots that maps $\lvert \lambda\rangle\lvert 0^u\rangle$ to $\lvert \lambda\rangle\lvert a_\lambda\rangle$ for any prescribed family of unit vectors $a_\lambda\in\mathbb C^{2^u}$. Its structure depends only on the register sizes.
  - Explanation: Conditional rotations prepare the magnitudes of each labelled state; a diagonal slot supplies its phases. The register sizes fix the skeleton while the target states determine its tables.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-slot-permutation)): Any family of permutations of the $2^u$ computational basis states can be implemented with $5u$ slots and $u$ temporary qubits that start and finish at zero. The skeleton is independent of the permutations.
  - Explanation: The permutation is written into temporary storage, its inverse erases the original index, and the registers are swapped. The temporary register is restored coherently.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-slot-fourier)): The unitary $$\lvert x\rangle\longmapsto 2^{-b/2}\sum_{z=0}^{2^b-1} e^{2\pi i xz/2^b}\lvert z\rangle$$ on $b\ge1$ qubits has a uniformly generated skeleton with $O(b)$ slots. Products of such transforms have a number of slots linear in their total register width.
  - Explanation: The positive-phase Fourier transform has a slot skeleton whose size grows linearly with register width. Products preserve this linear dependence on total width.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-contract-moment)): Let $L_1,\ldots,L_q$ be Hermitian $h\times h$ matrices, and let $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_q$ be independent Rademacher variables. Set $v=\lVert \sum_i L_i^2\rVert$. For every integer $a\geq1$, $$\label{contract:sign-bound} \mathbb E\lVert \sum_{i=1}^q\varepsilon_iL_i\rVert \leq 2h^{1/(2a)}\sqrt{(2a-1)v}.$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian moments control a Hermitian sign sum through the norm of its summed squares. Conditional averaging transfers the bound back to independent signs.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-contract-block)): For every unitary $U$ on $\mathbb C^d$, there are unitaries $P_{\mathrm L}$ and $P_{\mathrm R}$ on $\mathbb C^d$ such that $$\label{contract:block-form} V=P_{\mathrm L}UP_{\mathrm R} =\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\C&D\end{pmatrix}, \qquad \lVert D\rVert\leq r,$$ where the block sizes are $k,s$ from <a href="#contract:orders" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="contract:orders">[contract:orders]</a>. The transformations $P_{\mathrm L},P_{\mathrm R}$ and their inverses have implementations using $O(n)$ programmable slots and $O(n)$ clean working qubits, with a structure determined by $n,d$. The same structure works simultaneously for any family $U_\lambda$, using the label $\lambda$ as an additional control.
  - Explanation: Permutations, diagonal signs, and Walsh transforms expose a contracting internal block. Their skeletons preserve the valid subspace and can be controlled by labels.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-contract-depth)): For the parameters in <a href="#contract:orders" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="contract:orders">[contract:orders]</a>, $s\geq d/(4M)$. Starting at any $d\leq2^n$ and repeatedly replacing $d\geq2$ by $d-s$, one reaches $d=1$ in fewer than $$\label{contract:J} J=8M(n+1)$$ steps.
  - Explanation: The internal block removes a fixed polynomial fraction of the active dimension. Repeated reductions therefore terminate within the stated recursion bound.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fields-unitary)): For the unitary block matrix in <a href="#fields:block" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fields:block">[fields:block]</a> and any diagonal unitary $Z$, the matrix $F$ in <a href="#fields:feedback" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fields:feedback">[fields:feedback]</a> is unitary. If $$\label{fields:R} R=OF^*=\begin{pmatrix}I_k\\ Y\end{pmatrix}, \qquad Y=XF^*,$$ then $$\label{fields:exact} FE^*=R^*V, \qquad Y=(I-Z^*D^*)^{-1}Z^*B^*.$$
  - Explanation: Closing the internal output through diagonal phases gives a unitary port transfer matrix. Forward and backward feedback maps satisfy an exact intertwining identity.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-fields-gram)): For either $\mathcal T=\mathcal E_L$ or $\mathcal T=\mathcal R_L$, $$\label{fields:gram-bound} (1-r^{2L})I_d\ \le\ \mathcal T^*\mathcal T\ \le\ I_d.$$ In addition, let $\widetilde F$ apply $F(z)$ to the port register, controlled on $z$. Then $$\label{fields:intertwine} \lVert \widetilde F\mathcal E_L-\mathcal R_L V\rVert\le2\tau.$$
  - Explanation: Common-phase averaging cancels unequal degrees and makes the internal Gram sum telescope. Truncated forward and backward maps are contractions with a controlled intertwining error.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-fields-sparsity)): In these Fourier coordinates, every row of $\mathcal E_L$ and of $\mathcal R_L$ has at most $L$ nonzero entries.
  - Explanation: Power sums determine the multiset of internal visits encoded by a Fourier frequency. A nonzero coefficient can therefore involve only input indices in that multiset.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-encode-row-sparse)): Under <a href="#encode:assumptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="encode:assumptions">[encode:assumptions]</a>, an exact-slot unitary $\mathsf U_T$ satisfies $$\label{encode:generic-error} \lVert \mathsf U_T\iota-\jmath T\rVert\le5\varepsilon.$$ It uses $O(L(n+w+h))$ slots and $w+h+2$ fresh qubits. Its slot structure depends only on the register sizes, $d$, and $L$, and it works coherently for any family of matrices indexed by labels.
  - Explanation: Column preparation, conditional index erasure, and reflections amplify a scaled row-sparse matrix. Near-isometry makes the singular-direction errors uniform, including coherent labels.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-encode-implementation)): Under the hypotheses and definitions of Section <a href="#sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fields">4</a>, there are exact-slot unitaries $\mathsf U_E,\mathsf U_R$ such that $$\label{encode:field-error} \lVert \mathsf U_E\iota-\jmath\mathcal E_L\rVert\le5r^{2L}, \qquad \lVert \mathsf U_R\iota-\jmath\mathcal R_L\rVert\le5r^{2L}.$$ Each uses $O\bigl(L(K+n+h)+K\bigr)$ slots and $K+n+h+2$ fresh qubits, with slot structure independent of $V$. The construction works coherently with any additional label register.
  - Explanation: The row-sparse construction applies to both field maps. Fourier transforms then convert coefficient registers into phase registers without changing the approximation error.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rec-step)): Let $V$ be a unitary on $\mathbb C^{k+s}$ in the block form of Proposition <a href="#contract:block" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="contract:block">6</a>, and let $\lVert D\rVert\leq r<1$. Use the fields and encoders of Sections <a href="#sec:fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fields">4</a> and <a href="#sec:encoders" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:encoders">5</a> with truncation length $L$. Suppose a circuit synthesizes the family of $k$-dimensional unitaries $F(z)$, controlled by $z$, with clean-workspace operator norm error $\delta'$. Then one use of that circuit, preceded by $\mathsf U_E$ and followed by $\mathsf U_R^*$, synthesizes $V$ with clean-workspace error at most $$\label{rec:step-error} \delta'+\frac{2r^L}{1-r}+10r^{2L}.$$ The assertion also holds coherently for a family $V_\lambda$, with the smaller circuit controlled by both $\lambda$ and $z$.
  - Explanation: Forward encoding, a single smaller controlled synthesis, and reversed backward encoding implement a reduction. Encoder and truncation errors add to the smaller circuit error.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-rec-skeleton)): Let $n\geq1$, $1\leq d\leq2^n$, and $t\geq0$. There is a programmable-slot circuit structure, determined by $n,d,t$, with the following property. For every family $(U_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\{0,1\}^t}$ of unitaries on $\mathbb C^d$, its slot tables can be chosen so that the circuit implements the controlled unitary $U_\lambda$, preserves the labels, and has clean-workspace operator norm error less than $1/100$ in the sense of <a href="#slot:clean-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="slot:clean-error">[slot:clean-error]</a>. The circuit uses $$\label{rec:resources} t+O((n+1)^{10})\quad\text{qubits}, \qquad O((n+1)^{13})\quad\text{slots}.$$ Its structure, including each slot’s ordered control list, is generated deterministically in time polynomial in $n+t$ from $n,d,t$.
  - Explanation: A uniform generator assembles the recursive slot positions and controls without computing table contents. Tables encode the target family, while resources and accumulated error obey the stated bounds.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-compile-words)): There is an absolute positive integer $K_{\mathrm w}$ such that every $S\in SU(2)$ and every $0<\xi<1$ admit a word in $H,T,T^\dagger$ of length at most $K_{\mathrm w}\xi^{-3}$ whose matrix has determinant one and differs from $S$ by at most $\xi$ in operator norm.
  - Explanation: Determinant-one words are dense in the single-qubit special unitary group. Compactness and commutator refinement yield the stated polynomial approximation length.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-compile-slots)): Let a skeleton have $S$ slots on $Q$ qubits, and put $\eta=1/h$ for an integer $h\ge2$. There is a deterministic procedure, polynomial in $Q,S,1/\eta$, that produces a circuit over $H,T,T^\dagger,\mathrm{CNOT}$ and one Boolean oracle, with polynomially many qubits, gates, calls, and address bits. For every assignment of the slot tables, some contents of that oracle make the circuit differ from the prescribed skeleton by at most $S\eta$ on inputs with its added scratch qubits zero, with the comparison circuit keeping those scratch qubits zero.
  - Explanation: Oracle addresses retrieve gate symbols for each slot and control value. Phase scratch preserves relative phases, and an ideal-prefix telescoping estimate bounds total compilation error.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Boolean oracle** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A Boolean oracle $f:\{0,1\}^m\to\{0,1\}$ supplies the gate $$\label{intro:oracle} O_f\lvert x,b\rangle=\lvert x,b\oplus f(x)\rangle.$$
- **Output channel** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its output channel is obtained by retaining $n$ designated qubits and tracing out the remaining qubits.
- **Unitary channel** ([source](#intro:diamond)): For a unitary $U$, write $\mathcal U(\rho)=U\rho U^\dagger$.
- **Full diamond norm** ([source](#intro:diamond)): Our convention for channel error is the full diamond norm: if $\Phi$ is another channel on $n$ qubits, then $$\label{intro:diamond} \lVert \Phi-\mathcal U\rVert_\diamond =\sup_{\rho}\lVert \bigl((\Phi-\mathcal U)\otimes\mathrm{id}_R\bigr)(\rho)\rVert_1, \qquad \lVert X\rVert_1=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\sqrt{X^\dagger X},$$ where $R$ is an $n$-qubit reference register and $\rho$ ranges over density operators on the input and $R$.
- **Valid input subspace** ([source](#slot:clean-error)): For $1\le d\le 2^n$, write $$\mathcal H_d=\operatorname{span}\{\lvert 0\rangle,\ldots,\lvert d-1\rangle\} \subseteq (\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes n}.$$
- **Clean-workspace synthesis error** ([source](#slot:clean-error)): A circuit $C$ solves this controlled task with error $\delta$ if $$\label{slot:clean-error} \lVert C\iota-\iota\mathcal U\rVert\le\delta.$$
- **Programmable slot** ([source](#sec:slots)): A *programmable slot* specifies one target qubit and an ordered list of other qubits as controls. Given a table of matrices $W_y\in U(2)$, its action is $$\label{slot:definition} \sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^c}\lvert y\rangle\langle y\rvert\otimes W_y,$$ where $c$ is the number of controls.
- **Circuit skeleton** ([source](#sec:slots)): A *circuit skeleton* specifies the registers, targets, and control lists of its slots, but leaves their tables unspecified.
- **Rademacher variable** ([source](#sec:contraction)): A *Rademacher variable* takes the values $1$ and $-1$ with equal probability.
- **Feedback matrices** ([source](#fields:feedback)): Since $\lVert DZ\rVert<1$, the second equation has the unique solution $v=Xu$, and the first then gives $y=Fu$, where $$\label{fields:feedback} X=(I-DZ)^{-1}C,\qquad F=A+BZX, \qquad E=\begin{pmatrix}I_k\\ ZX\end{pmatrix},\qquad O=\begin{pmatrix}F\\ X\end{pmatrix}.$$
- **Phase group** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Fix an integer truncation length $L\ge1$, and set $$\label{fields:parameters} b=nL+\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil,\qquad \mathcal G=(\mathbb Z/(2^b))^{L+1}.$$
- **Power-sum phase assignment** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Number the internal coordinates by $i=1,\ldots,s$ and put $$\label{fields:phases} v_i=(1,i,i^2,\ldots,i^L)\in\mathcal G, \qquad Z(z)=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\bigl(\chi_{v_1}(z),\ldots,\chi_{v_s}(z)\bigr).$$
- **Field maps** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Define the field maps $\mathcal E_L,\mathcal R_L: \mathbb C^d\longrightarrow\mathbb C^{\mathcal G}\otimes\mathbb C^k$ by $$\label{fields:maps} \mathcal E_L x=\frac1{\sqrt{\|\mathcal G\|}} \sum_{z\in\mathcal G}\lvert z\rangle\otimes E_L(z)^*x, \qquad \mathcal R_L x=\frac1{\sqrt{\|\mathcal G\|}} \sum_{z\in\mathcal G}\lvert z\rangle\otimes R_L(z)^*x.$$

The circuit uses the gates $H,T,T^\\dagger,\\mathrm{CNOT}$ and a single Boolean oracle of input length $m(n)\\le p(n)$.         {
          "anchor": "fields:tails",
          "exact_quote": "Apply Lemma <a href=\"#fields:unitary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fields:unitary\">8</a> at each $z$ to obtain $F(z),E(z),R(z)$. Truncate the two Neumann expansions by defining $$\\label{fields:truncations}\n E_L(z)=\\begin{pmatrix}\n I_k\\\\[2pt] \\displaystyle\\sum_{\\ell=0}^{L-1}Z(DZ)^\\ell C\n \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n R_L(z)=\\begin{pmatrix}\n I_k\\\\[2pt] \\displaystyle\\sum_{\\ell=0}^{L-1}(Z^*D^*)^\\ell Z^*B^*\n \\end{pmatrix}.$$ Here and below, products containing $Z$ are evaluated at the same $z$. Since every block of a unitary has norm at most one, $$\\label{fields:tails}\n \\lVert E_L(z)-E(z)\\rVert,\\ \\lVert R_L(z)-R(z)\\rVert\n \\le \\tau,\\qquad \\tau=\\frac{r^L}{1-r}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-283.1"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#encode:assumptions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"encode:assumptions\">[encode:assumptions]</a>, an exact-slot unitary $\\mathsf U_T$ satisfies $$\\label{encode:generic-error}\n \\lVert \\mathsf U_T\\iota-\\jmath T\\rVert\\le5\\varepsilon.$$ It uses $O(L(n+w+h))$ slots and $w+h+2$ fresh qubits. Its slot structure depends only on the register sizes, $d$, and $L$, and it works coherently for any family of matrices indexed by labels.",
          "source_id": "PM-283.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-encode-implementation",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses and definitions of Section <a href=\"#sec:fields\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:fields\">4</a>, there are exact-slot unitaries $\\mathsf U_E,\\mathsf U_R$ such that $$\\label{encode:field-error}\n \\lVert \\mathsf U_E\\iota-\\jmath\\mathcal E_L\\rVert\\le5r^{2L},\n \\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathsf U_R\\iota-\\jmath\\mathcal R_L\\rVert\\le5r^{2L}.$$ Each uses $O\\bigl(L(K+n+h)+K\\bigr)$ slots and $K+n+h+2$ fresh qubits, with slot structure independent of $V$.           "source_id": "PM-283.1"
        }

    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-step",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a unitary on $\\mathbb C^{k+s}$ in the block form of Proposition <a href=\"#contract:block\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"contract:block\">6</a>, and let $\\lVert D\\rVert\\leq r<1$. Use the fields and encoders of Sections <a href=\"#sec:fields\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:fields\">4</a> and <a href=\"#sec:encoders\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:encoders\">5</a> with truncation length $L$. Suppose a circuit synthesizes the family of $k$-dimensional unitaries $F(z)$, controlled by $z$, with clean-workspace operator norm error $\\delta'$. Then one use of that circuit, preceded by $\\mathsf U_E$ and followed by $\\mathsf U_R^*$, synthesizes $V$ with clean-workspace error at most $$\\label{rec:step-error}\n \\delta'+\\frac{2r^L}{1-r}+10r^{2L}.$$ The assertion also holds coherently for a family $V_\\lambda$, with the smaller circuit controlled by both $\\lambda$ and $z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-283.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "Q14",
      "kind": "bound",

    {
      "computational_model": "Programmable slots",
      "effectivity": "partially_effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rec-skeleton",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\geq1$, $1\\leq d\\leq2^n$, and $t\\geq0$. There is a programmable-slot circuit structure, determined by $n,d,t$, with the following property. For every family $(U_\\lambda)_{\\lambda\\in\\{0,1\\}^t}$ of unitaries on $\\mathbb C^d$, its slot tables can be chosen so that the circuit implements the controlled unitary $U_\\lambda$, preserves the labels, and has clean-workspace operator norm error less than $1/100$ in the sense of <a href=\"#slot:clean-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"slot:clean-error\">[slot:clean-error]</a>. The circuit uses $$\\label{rec:resources}\n t+O((n+1)^{10})\\quad\\text{qubits},\n \\qquad O((n+1)^{13})\\quad\\text{slots}.$$ Its structure, including each slot’s ordered control list, is generated deterministically in time polynomial in $n+t$ from $n,d,t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-283.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "Q15",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "Skeleton error",
      "regime": {

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compile-slots-2): Combine uniform recursive synthesis with oracle slot compilation and convert isometry error into channel error. -> C1
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-slot-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slot-preparation): Prepare conditional magnitudes and then phases. -> C2
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-slot-permutation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slot-permutation): Write the image, erase the preimage, and swap registers. -> C3
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-slot-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-slot-fourier): Process input bits with controlled phases and reverse output order. -> C4
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-contract-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-contract-moment): Bound Gaussian trace moments, then average magnitudes conditional on signs. -> C5
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-contract-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-contract-block): Choose signs through entry tails and Hermitian moment bounds. -> C6
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-contract-depth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-contract-depth): Bound each dimension decrease by a common contraction factor. -> C7
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-fields-unitary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-unitary): Solve the feedback equation and compare conserved input and output norms. -> C8
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-fields-gram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-gram): Average common phases and telescope the internal Gram sum. -> C9
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-fields-sparsity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-sparsity): Recover multisets from their power sums using symmetric polynomial identities. -> C10
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-encode-row-sparse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-encode-row-sparse): Prepare columns, erase row supports, and amplify singular directions. -> C11
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-encode-implementation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-encode-implementation): Apply the row-sparse encoder in Fourier coordinates and transform its phase registers. -> C12
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-rec-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-step): Insert the smaller synthesis between encoders and add norm errors. -> C13
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-rec-skeleton) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rec-skeleton): Recurse on dimension, count shared encoder layouts, and sum errors. -> C14
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-compile-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compile-words): Use dense determinant-one words, compactness, and commutator refinement. -> C15
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-compile-slots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compile-slots): Look up gate symbols coherently and telescope errors on ideal prefixes. -> C16

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-multiset**: A Fourier frequency determines its multiset of internal indices, including repeated visits.
- **I-nowrap**: Reduction of a power-sum tuple modulo the phase-register modulus preserves its ordinary integer coordinates.
- **I-uniform**: The same uniformly generated circuit works for every target unitary after a suitable target-dependent oracle is chosen.
- **I-clean**: The controlled approximation includes restoration of workspace and coherence across labels.
- **I-phase**: Programmable constructions retain branch scalar phases under coherent control.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-oracle**: Treat the theorem as an efficient classical compiler from an arbitrary target description. -> Uniform circuit generation does not supply the target-dependent oracle contents.
- **A-phase**: Discard scalar phases independently in each label branch. -> Branch phases become observable relative phases on coherent label superpositions.
- **A-width**: Infer polynomial total resources from a constant query count alone. -> A wide oracle interface can consume exponential resources despite a small query count.
- **A-proxy**: Treat a finite multiset reconstruction check as verification of the full synthesis theorem. -> The check does not establish norm estimates, coherent encoder implementation or oracle compilation.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The Fourier sparsity proof reconstructs finite internal-index multisets from their power sums using Newton’s identities. This implements that combinatorial ingredient; the synthesis theorem and its analytic estimates remain separate proof obligations.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integers n,L,s,sign.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1<=n<=3, 1<=L<=3, 1<=s<=2**n, sign in {-1,1}; bounded verification domain.
# OUTPUTS: Exact signed modular frequencies and Newton-decoded multisets, including the empty multiset.
# TIME: O(binomial(s+L,L)*(L*s+L**2)) exact arithmetic operations, excluding integer bit costs.
# SPACE: O(binomial(s+L,L)*L) integers for the returned records, excluding integer bit costs.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpz power sums, integer Newton identities and synthetic polynomial division.
# FAILURE: Invalid input returns an error object; failed divisibility or reconstruction raises an exception.
import json
import sys
from itertools import combinations_with_replacement
from flint import fmpz


def recover(frequency, n, L, s, sign, modulus):
    powers = [(sign * x) % modulus for x in frequency]
    m = powers[0]
    if m > L:
        raise ValueError('degree outside truncation')
    elementary = [fmpz(1)]
    for j in range(1, m + 1):
        numerator = sum(((-1) ** (t-1)) * elementary[j-t] * fmpz(powers[t]) for t in range(1, j+1))
        if numerator % j:
            raise ValueError('Newton division is not integral')
        elementary.append(numerator // j)
    coefficients = [((-1) ** j) * elementary[j] for j in range(m+1)]
    recovered = []
    for root in range(1, s+1):
        while len(coefficients) > 1:
            quotient = [coefficients[0]]
            for coefficient in coefficients[1:-1]:
                quotient.append(coefficient + root * quotient[-1])
            remainder = coefficients[-1] + root * quotient[-1]
            if remainder:
                break
            recovered.append(root)
            coefficients = quotient
    if len(coefficients) != 1 or len(recovered) != m:
        raise ValueError('polynomial does not split over the permitted indices')
    for j in range(L+1):
        if sum(fmpz(i) ** j for i in recovered) != powers[j]:
            raise ValueError('exact power-sum recheck failed')
    return recovered


def execute(data):
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'n','L','s','sign'}:
        return {'error': 'invalid_input'}
    n, L, s, sign = (data[k] for k in ('n','L','s','sign'))
    if any(type(x) is not int for x in (n,L,s,sign)) or not (1 <= n <= 3 and 1 <= L <= 3 and 1 <= s <= 2**n and sign in (-1,1)):
        return {'error': 'invalid_input'}
    b = n * L + L.bit_length()
    modulus = 2 ** b
    rows = []
    for degree in range(L+1):
        for indices in combinations_with_replacement(range(1,s+1), degree):
            frequency = [int(sign * sum(fmpz(i) ** j for i in indices) % modulus) for j in range(L+1)]
            recovered = recover(frequency,n,L,s,sign,modulus)
            rows.append({'frequency':frequency,'multiset':recovered})
    return {'b': b, 'rows': rows}

try:
    result = execute(json.loads(sys.stdin.read()))
except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError, KeyError):
    result = {'error':'invalid_input'}
print(json.dumps(result,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The uniform circuit generator supplies a theoretical basis for quantum circuit-generation software when the target-dependent oracle is supplied separately. The paper gives no efficient procedure to construct that oracle. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a game engine or a classical simulation algorithm. The result concerns quantum unitary channels with coherent Boolean-oracle access. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Slot compilation can inform a quantum circuit-generation tool that emits coherent oracle lookups while preserving relative phases. Selection of approximating words remains an existence requirement for the oracle contents. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper states a uniform quantum oracle algorithm for constant-error unitary synthesis, with polynomial circuit generation and quantum resources. Its guarantee is conditional on a suitable target-dependent Boolean oracle. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Power-sum phase coordinates provide an exact representation of bounded multisets and restrict the possible input columns of a Fourier row. Adapting this representation to classical data structures requires separate operational bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: physical layout, device timing, noise tolerance, or accelerator design. Polynomial abstract gate and qubit bounds alone do not supply a hardware implementation contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a security definition, adversarial model, or cryptographic construction. Prior cryptographic literature is cited as background to query lower bounds. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a learning model, training objective, or data-driven construction of the oracle. Target-dependent oracle existence is not a learning procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an optimisation objective or operations-research algorithm. The sign choices and slot tables are existence prescriptions for quantum synthesis. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: communication, consensus, or distributed execution guarantees. Coherent quantum label control does not provide a classical coordination protocol. |
| other (quantum_unitary_synthesis_and_query_complexity) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Quantum unitary synthesis and quantum query complexity are the direct setting: a uniform circuit works for every target unitary after suitable Boolean-oracle contents are chosen. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (nonconstructive): Uniform generation of the circuit does not supply an efficient classical construction of the target-dependent oracle. Blocked use: ['Treating the theorem as an efficient classical algorithm that constructs the oracle from a target unitary.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Polynomial circuit resources do not bound the oracle truth-table size or its classical circuit complexity. Blocked use: ['Charging the oracle description or its classical implementation to the stated polynomial resource bound.']
- **L3** (approximation): The main result supplies constant-error channel synthesis; an arbitrary-precision channel guarantee is not stated. Blocked use: ['Citing the main theorem as an arbitrary-precision channel synthesis guarantee.']
- **L4** (formal_scope): The channel error uses the full diamond norm with an input and reference register, rather than a phase-insensitive state comparison. Blocked use: ['Replacing the channel criterion by an error on a selected input state.']
- **L5** (edge_case): The controlled synthesis estimate includes restoration of workspace and coherence across labels, but prescribes no action outside the valid input subspace. Blocked use: ['Claiming the approximation holds on arbitrary initial workspace or invalid input indices.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): Scalar phases are retained because control labels turn them into relative phases. Blocked use: ['Discarding label-dependent scalar phases during gate compilation.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The feedback construction assumes a unitary block matrix with a strictly contracting internal block. Blocked use: ['Applying the inverse geometric-series construction when the internal block is not strictly contracting.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The encoder requires row sparsity and a nearly isometric Gram matrix; row sparsity does not imply column sparsity. Blocked use: ['Replacing row sparsity by column sparsity alone.', 'Treating arbitrary sparse matrices as satisfying the nearly isometric encoder hypotheses.']
- **L9** (nonconstructive): The random-sign argument establishes the existence of suitable slot tables; it does not make the uniform generator select their contents. Blocked use: ['Reading the probabilistic existence argument as an efficient algorithm for choosing the target-dependent tables.']
- **L10** (nonconstructive): The gate-word bound uses a fixed integer whose existence is proved; the uniform generator does not select approximating words. Blocked use: ['Assuming the generator computes gate words for the target table entries.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a positive answer to the constant-error formulation of the Aaronson–Kuperberg unitary synthesis problem. For every <i>n</i>, a quantum oracle circuit generated in polynomial time from <i>n</i> alone can approximate the channel of every <i>n</i>-qubit unitary to full diamond-norm error at most 1/2, after a suitable Boolean oracle is chosen. Using the fixed gates $`H,T,T^\dagger,\mathrm{CNOT}`$, the circuit has polynomially many qubits, elementary gates, and oracle calls, and its oracle queries have polynomial length. The oracle may depend on the target unitary; the theorem does not give an efficient classical procedure for constructing it.
